=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given N strings S_1,S_2,\ldots,S_N. Each string consists of lowercase English letters.
For each k=1,2,\ldots,N, solve the following problem.

Let T=S_k and consider performing the following two types of operations any number of times in any order:

- Pay a cost of 1 to delete the last character of T. This operation is possible when T is not empty.
- Pay a cost of 1 to add any lowercase English letter to the end of T.

Find the minimum total cost needed to make T either empty or match one of S_1,S_2,\ldots,S_{k-1}.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
S_1
S_2
\vdots
S_N

Output

Print N lines.
The i-th line (1\le i\le N) should contain the answer for k=i.

Constraints


- 1\le N\le 2\times 10^5
- Each S_i is a string of length at least 1 consisting of lowercase English letters.
- \displaystyle \sum_{i=1}^N |S_i|\le 2\times 10^5

Sample Input 1

3
snuke
snuki
snuuk

Sample Output 1

5
2
4

For k=1, you can make T empty by performing the delete operation five times.
For k=2, you can make T match S_1 by deleting the last character and then adding e to the end.
For k=3, you can make T match S_2 by deleting the last character twice, then adding k to the end, and finally adding i to the end.

Sample Input 2

3
abc
arc
agc

Sample Output 2

3
3
3

Sample Input 3

8
at
atatat
attat
aatatatt
attattat
ttatta
tta
tt

Sample Output 3

2
4
3
8
3
6
3
1

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Implementation:

import sys
def main():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    if not data: return
    N = int(data[0])
    strings = data[1:]
    children = [{}]  # list of dict
    min_len = [10**18]  # root initially no inserted? But root min should be INF until insert. We can initialize INF.
    out=[]
    INF=10**18
    for s in strings[:N]:
        # query
        ans = INF
        node = 0
        depth = 0
        # root candidate
        if min_len[0] != INF:
            ans = min_len[0]  # -0
        # traverse
        # Need for each char, if child exists, update ans.
        # But if root min INF (no previous), skip. For k=1 ans INF.
        for b in s:
            nxt = children[node].get(b)
            if nxt is None:
                break
            node = nxt
            depth += 1
            val = min_len[node] - 2*depth
            if val < ans:
                ans = val
        # answer
        if ans < 0:
            res = len(s) + ans
        else:
            res = len(s)
        out.append(str(res))
        # insert s
        node = 0
        m = len(s)
        if m < min_len[0]:
            min_len[0] = m
        for b in s:
            nxt = children[node].get(b)
            if nxt is None:
                nxt = len(children)
                children[node][b] = nxt
                children.append({})
                min_len.append(INF)
            node = nxt
            if m < min_len[node]:
                min_len[node] = m
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

Let's adjust code:

children = [{}]
min_len = [0]  # empty target
for s in strings:
    # query
    ans = 0
    node = 0
    depth = 0
    for b in s:
        nxt = children[node].get(b)
        if nxt is None:
            break
        node = nxt
        depth += 1
        val = min_len[node] - 2*depth
        if val < ans:
            ans = val
    out.append(str(len(s) + ans))
    # insert
    node = 0
    m = len(s)
    # no need update root? root min 0, m>0 so no.
    for b in s:
        nxt = children[node].get(b)
        if nxt is None:
            nxt = len(children)
            children[node][b] = nxt
            children.append({})
            min_len.append(10**18)
        node = nxt
        if m < min_len[node]:
            min_len[node] = m

Conversely, for any v on path and x that achieves best[v], x in subtree(v), lcp(s,x) >= depth(v), so cost_x = |x|-2lcp <= |x|-2depth(v)=best[v]. Thus min_x cost <= ans. Equality. This is same as before but stores best directly. This is more efficient and elegant.

Implementation with best:

import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    if not data: return
    N = int(data[0])
    children = [{}]
    depth = [0]
    best = [0]  # empty string target
    out = []
    for i in range(1, N+1):
        s = data[i]
        # query
        ans = 0
        node = 0
        for b in s:
            nxt = children[node].get(b)
            if nxt is None:
                break
            node = nxt
            if best[node] < ans:
                ans = best[node]
        out.append(str(len(s) + ans))
        # insert
        node = 0
        m = len(s)
        d = 0
        for b in s:
            nxt = children[node].get(b)
            if nxt is None:
                nxt = len(children)
                children[node][b] = nxt
                children.append({})
                depth.append(d + 1)
                best.append(10**18)
            node = nxt
            d = depth[node]
            val = m - 2*d
            if val < best[node]:
                best[node] = val
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

Wait, in insertion loop, we set `d = depth[node]` after moving. But for a newly created node, depth appended as d+1 where d was depth of parent. Then after moving, `d = depth[node]` is correct. For existing node, depth known. Good.

But note: In insertion loop, we use variable `d` for depth of current node. Initially d=0. For each char, child depth = d+1. If child exists, we could set d = depth[child] or d+1. Since depth[child] should equal d+1. But if we use `d = depth[node]` after moving, okay. However, for a newly created node, we append depth d+1. Then `d = depth[node]` gets d+1. Good.

For example node depth1, inserted string length3, best=1. But root best0, so query ans remains0 unless deeper negative. But best positive is okay. If we had initialized best INF and only update if val < best, positive val will set. Good. If we wanted best to represent min over inserted strings, positive values are allowed. So no issue.

But wait, for root best=0. For non-root, best can be positive. If a node has best positive, query ans may ignore due to root0. But if all candidates positive, empty target best0 is min. Good.

Streaming version:
import sys
input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
N = int(input())
children = [{}]
best = [0]
out = []
for _ in range(N):
    s = input().strip()
    # query...
    # insert...
sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

This avoids storing all strings. `strip()` removes newline; since strings lowercase no spaces, okay. But `strip()` also removes whitespace; strings don't have leading/trailing spaces. Good. It returns bytes. This is memory efficient. Time okay. Use this in final? It is slightly more code but fine. Need be careful if there are blank lines? Input format no blank lines. `strip()` would remove all whitespace, but strings are lowercase, so okay. If a string could be "a\n", strip -> b'a'. Good. If string length could be 0, strip would b'' but constraints no. Good.

Using `readline` in loop N times is fine for 2e5. `strip()` creates a copy? It returns new bytes without newline. Fine. Could use `rstrip(b'\n')` to avoid removing spaces (none). `strip()` okay. If using `rstrip(b'\n')`, if Windows CRLF, leaves \r? AtCoder uses LF. `strip()` handles both. Good.

Streaming code:
import sys

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N_line = input()
    if not N_line:
        return
    N = int(N_line)
    children = [{}]
    best = [0]
    out = []
    INF = 10**18
    for _ in range(N):
        s = input().strip()
        ans = 0
        node = 0
        for b in s:
            nxt = children[node].get(b)
            if nxt is None:
                break
            node = nxt
            if best[node] < ans:
                ans = best[node]
        out.append(str(len(s) + ans))
        node = 0
        d = 0
        m = len(s)
        for b in s:
            nxt = children[node].get(b)
            if nxt is None:
                nxt = len(children)
                children[node][b] = nxt
                children.append({})
                best.append(INF)
            node = nxt
            d += 1
            val = m - 2 * d
            if val < best[node]:
                best[node] = val
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

This is good. But note: If input line could be empty due to trailing newline? The loop reads exactly N lines. Good.

Now, let's consider if
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N = int(input())

    children = [{}]
    best = [0]  # best[0] = 0 represents making T empty

    ans_list = []
    INF = 10**18

    for _ in range(N):
        s = input().strip()

        # Query
        ans = 0
        node = 0
        for ch in s:
            nxt = children[node].get(ch)
            if nxt is None:
                break
            node = nxt
            if best[node] < ans:
                ans = best[node]

        ans_list.append(str(len(s) + ans))

        # Insert
        node = 0
        depth = 0
        m = len(s)
        for ch in s:
            nxt = children[node].get(ch)
            if nxt is None:
                nxt = len(children)
                children[node][ch] = nxt
                children.append({})
                best.append(INF)
            node = nxt
            depth += 1
            val = m - 2 * depth
            if val < best[node]:
                best[node] = val

    print("\n".join(ans_list))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
